Chapter 458: Raid (3)

To truly enjoy [Dungeon and Stone], you need to be adaptable.

I'm not talking about flexible essence builds, but the basic gameplay itself.

You might be hunting monsters, and suddenly a rift opens. You might encounter looters, or be betrayed by your own teammate.

These unexpected events can happen at any moment.

Like right now.

"Mister, this..."

"Yeah, that's right."

The Floor Lord had been summoned.

It meant that someone on the 3rd floor had placed seven corpses in the cabin.

Whether it was intentional or not, I couldn't be sure.

Well, I was pretty sure it was intentional...

'But... there's always a chance it was an accident.'

After all, I had experienced it firsthand.

Misha and I were the ones who had accidentally summoned the Floor Lord in the past.

We had cremated the corpses in the cabin because we needed a place to rest... but someone had already cremated some corpses before us, and the conditions for summoning the Floor Lord had been met.

Anyway, that wasn't important right now.

"Yandel, what are we going to do?"

"We're leaving the forest. We can't go to the 4th floor anyway."

When the Chaos Lord, Riakis, was summoned, all the portals on the 3rd floor were deactivated.

The entire 3rd floor became a boss room.

"I'll take care of them."

Erwen used her fire spirit to eliminate the three Chaos Spirits that were blocking our path.

「The Chaos Spirits have been destroyed.」

「The area is now corrupted.」

We didn't have to worry about tile management this time.

We weren't planning to fight Riakis.

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

We heard a familiar howl coming from the Witch's Forest.

I felt a pang of regret.

I had thought we would finally settle the score with Riakis this time...

"Bjorn! We can't just run away! We have to do something!"

"What do you want us to do? Fight the Floor Lord with just the four of us?"

Technically, there were five of us, including Auyen... but no one bothered to correct me.

It wasn't like adding one more person would change the outcome.

As we were retreating from the forest, we saw them.

"Bjorn! There's something creepy coming this way! What is that?!"

"It's a Chaos Spirit."

When Riakis was summoned, these token monsters would spawn all over the 3rd floor.

They would chase their target until one of them died.

'It's been a while since I've seen these guys...'

"Bjorn, that's not what I meant... I'm saying we have to do something. Didn't you say a lot of people died last time?"

She was right.

I had heard about the incident.

I felt guilty because I was partly responsible for it.

"And besides, I heard that high-level explorers have a duty to protect lower-level explorers during emergencies!"

Ah, the Explorer's Oath.

That was why the Djarwi Clan had protected the lower-level explorers during the Chaos Lord incident.

Larger clans were more sensitive to the guild's opinion.

'Well, they still took our money...'

But one thing was certain.

If it weren't for the Djarwi Clan, there would have been many more casualties. They had protected the lower-level explorers for a whole day, and even held off Riakis for almost four hours after he appeared.

"I-I don't know how to fight, but... you... you can do something, right?"

"Ainar, calm down."

I stopped walking and turned around, and Ainar stopped talking. She looked anxious, but she didn't avoid my gaze.

I could see it in her eyes.

"...."

Ainar was a righteous person.

She only showed her ruthless side to monsters and looters.

She was kind and compassionate towards others, and always willing to help if she could.

And...

"Don't worry. I'm not just going to stand here and do nothing."

I didn't see that as a weakness.

I had learned something from her.

Sometimes, kindness wasn't a weakness.

"Oh! I knew it!"

"Did you think I would just ignore our fellow barbarians? There must be a lot of them on the 3rd floor."

I had said that, but even if it wasn't about my tribe, I wouldn't have ignored the situation.

My fame was at stake.

If I was known for running away from a disaster, my reputation would plummet.

Therefore...

"Ooooooh!! So what are we going to do?! I'll follow you, even if you tell me to jump into fire!"

"We'll observe the situation for now."

"...Huh?"

We stayed at a safe distance, watching the forest.

"We might not have to intervene?"

"The Explorer's Guild rarely approves requests to slay the 3rd-floor Floor Lord. Maybe some clan summoned it without permission."

"Ah! So if they succeed, there's no problem!"

"That's right."

Of course, it was just speculation.

We didn't have any concrete information.

Therefore...

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

We listened to the howls echoing through the forest and asked Erwen for updates.

"Riakis is heading west. It's too far to see clearly, but there are people around him."

"How many?"

"I don't know. It's too far..."

"Then we should get closer."

We moved along the outskirts of the forest, heading west. Erwen's exceptional detection skills gave us more information.

"...There are about fifteen of them. It seems like they're being chased by Riakis."

Well, it looked more like they were leading him to a suitable location for a raid. The Witch's Forest, with its dense trees and foliage, wasn't ideal for a raid.

I was more interested in something else.

"Fifteen..."

It was too few for a raid.

Well, they could probably slay him with a well-organized raid party...

"It's more likely that they're a decoy, and the main force is waiting somewhere else."

"That makes sense."

"Can you hear what they're saying?"

"No, I can't. They're probably using the 'Voice Control' spell."

"Hmm, I see."

It was a shame, but it was also a relief.

If it had been a genuine emergency, I would have had to intervene and tank the damage.

Pitter-patter, pitter-patter.

It started to rain.

Sizzle!

Black rain, corrosive enough to burn my skin on contact.

It was Riakis's passive aura skill.

「Character is taking continuous damage from Chaos attribute.」

「Character's Magic Resistance is greatly reduced.」

「Warning: Continuous exposure to Chaos attribute damage may inflict the [Confusion] status effect.」

It meant that we were close to Riakis.

"We should move back."

We couldn't resist his passive aura without a priest, so we adjusted our distance based on the rain.

And after a while...

"They're coming out of the forest!"

The darkness that had been absorbing the light dissipated as we reached the edge of the 3rd floor.

I didn't have Erwen or Amelia's exceptional eyesight, but I could still see a group of fifteen explorers running towards us.

Kaboom! Kaboom! Kaboom!

And of course, a giant monster was chasing them.

"Erwen, check their clan emblem."

"...They don't have one."

What? They removed their emblems?

It made sense.

They were probably slaying the Floor Lord without permission.

"They're heading this way!"

They ran past us, leading Riakis away, and Amelia asked,

"Yandel, what are we going to do?"

"We're following them."

"Why?"

"If they fail, it'll be a disaster."

And who knows?

Maybe we could join the raid.

We followed the decoy group and arrived at the 'Thornreed Field'.

'This is the ideal location.'

It was the perfect place for a raid, with its flat, open terrain and no obstacles.

They had even burned down the tall thornreeds, preparing the battlefield.

But...

"Mister, there's the main for—"

Suddenly, Erwen stopped talking.

"What happened?"

"They detected us. Someone's approaching."

"Can you outrun them?"

"...It'll be difficult. There's only one of them."

Hmm, I see.

I made my decision.

"Then we'll wait here."

There was no reason to run away.

I was also curious.

Who were these people who had summoned the Floor Lord without permission?

"He's here."

A figure appeared from the burned-out field, running towards us.

He stopped about 10 meters away.

He had seven daggers on his belt.

"...."

"...."

He looked at us, his face hardening.

He didn't say a word.

So I spoke first.

"Why are you so quiet? You were in such a hurry to get here."

"...It seems we have the honor of meeting a celebrity."

Ah, so that's why he was making that face.

He was intimidated.

"It's a pleasure to meet you, Baron Yandel. I never expected to see someone like you on the 3rd floor—"

"Just tell me who you are."

I cut him off and asked, and he hesitated for a moment before answering.

"...My name is Malid Kevron."

I wasn't sure if it was his real name, but that's how he introduced himself.

I asked him another question.

"What clan are you from?"

"...We're from the Sawtooth Clan."

"The Sawtooth Clan..."

One of the four largest clans in the city, the same clan that James Kala had been the vice-captain of.

"Interesting. It's more surprising to see you here than it is to see me on the 3rd floor."

I chuckled, and he boldly asked,

"Did you summon the Floor Lord, Baron?"

He was a sharp one.

He wasn't afraid to take the initiative.

"I didn't."

"I see."

"But you're going to deny it if I ask you the same question, right?"

"We didn't summon it."

"So you're saying the group leading Riakis here has nothing to do with you?"

"They are our members. But we were just nearby, and we're trying to resolve the situation according to the Explorer's Oath."

Nice try.

"Then that's good. We'll help you."

I offered him a helping hand, and his face hardened.

"...We appreciate your offer, Baron, but we don't need your help."

"Why not?"

"This is a dangerous task, best left to explorers."

So he was looking down on me because I was a noble?

I almost lost it...

"The other nobles wouldn't want to see the Baron's reputation tarnished by such a reckless act."

"...What?"

What did I just hear?

"Are you talking about Count Alminus? One of the Sawtooth Clan's sponsors?"

I asked to confirm, and he smirked.

"Yes, that's right."

He was serious.

There was no point in being polite anymore.

"What the hell are you talking about, you little punk?"

"...I beg your pardon?"

He looked at me, stunned, and I took a step forward.

"Hey, Dagger."

"...Yes?"

"Is your head filled with goblin shit?"

"I... I don't understand..."

He was sweating, backing away.

"Well, we'll find out soon enough. What's inside your head, that is."

I raised my hammer.

Chapter 459: Raid (4)

As soon as I raised my hammer, I activated [Transcendence] and [Eye of the Storm], pulling the dagger user towards me.

Swaaaaaaaaaa!

The ambush was successful. His frail body was helplessly dragged towards me.

"......!"

I reached out and grabbed his neck.

At that moment, Amelia called out to me.

"Yandel."

Her voice was filled with worry.

If she thought I was actually going to kill him, I was a little offended.

I wouldn't crush his head over something like this.

I'm not a real barbarian.

Squeeze.

This was just a performance.

A slightly... emotional one.

"Mister, they're almost here."

As expected, as soon as I grabbed his neck, the other explorers appeared in the distance. It didn't take long for them to reach us.

"...What is the meaning of this, Baron Yandel?!"

They already knew my name.

It wasn't surprising.

They must have been listening to our conversation through a message stone or something.

"Cough...!"

I tightened my grip, and the dagger user struggled even more.

"Release him this instant!"

"Why should I?"

"Even if you're a noble, there are lines you cannot cross! How dare you threaten an innocent explorer without any reason?! Do you think this will be tolerated?!"

His words were absurd.

First of all, they were the ones who had summoned the Floor Lord without permission.

And secondly...

"Who said he was innocent?"

I spoke clearly, loud enough for everyone to hear.

"He's a traitor. He dared to threaten and insult a noble of Lafdonia!"

"W-What?! That's ridiculous! He would never—"

"What? Are you insulting me too?"

"N-No, that's not what I meant..."

The dagger user struggled even more as our conversation continued.

"Cough... Urgh...!"

He was quite persistent.

He should have drawn his dagger and stabbed me by now.

'He knows that would only make things worse.'

"L-Let's talk this over. There must be some misunderstanding. We can resolve this peacefully if we just—"

I didn't think he would attack me, even if I provoked him further.

So I threw him back to his comrades.

"V-Vice-Captain! Are you alright?"

Huh... he was the vice-captain?

The one who replaced James Kala.

'No wonder he was so persistent...'

His loyalty to the clan was admirable, but that didn't make me like him or the Sawtooth Clan any more.

"Alright, explain yourselves. What was the misunderstanding?"

I asked the explorer who had been shouting at me, but the dagger user intervened, covering his mouth.

And...

"We sincerely apologize for disturbing Baron Yandel. Please forgive us."

He apologized without any excuses.

"Mentioning Count Alminus was a grave mistake on our part."

He was a sharp one.

He knew exactly how to avoid the traps I had laid.

"If you wish to punish us, we will accept any judgment you deem fit upon our return to the city."

That made it difficult for me to continue.

He hadn't insulted me directly, and his threats were veiled.

I couldn't demand his execution from 'Mozelan' with just this.

'And Count Alminus's influence will be much stronger in the city.'

I might even be accused of abusing my power if I pushed this further.

Therefore...

"So you're still going to do it?"

I decided to postpone the judgment and asked again.

After all, what I wanted wasn't an apology or punishment.

"I'm also an explorer, and I want to help resolve this situation as quickly as possible."

"...I see."

"So are you still planning to stop me?"

The dagger user thought for a moment and then answered.

"If I may be so bold, what are your plans once this situation is resolved?"

He was asking about the loot distribution.

"We're all explorers, aren't we? We'll roll the dice and decide fairly."

"...As expected."

The dagger user sighed and then spoke.

"Then we'll withdraw."

"...What?"

"There's a saying that a teammate who doesn't work well with you is more dangerous than an enemy. And besides, we can't stop you, can we, Baron Yandel? If we fight alongside you, we'll only hinder your progress and cause more casualties."

"So you're just going to give up?"

"Yes. We trust you, the hero known as Baron Yandel."

Huh, this punk...

"We'll fulfill our duty by helping the other lower-level explorers on the 3rd floor."

He was basically saying he would rather flip the table than share the loot.

'He's really determined to protect his share...'

Unfortunately, I couldn't stop him.

If this got out, I would be accused of abusing my power and driving them away.

'They'll definitely spin the story that way.'

"What are your orders?"

I had no choice.

"I'll leave Riakis to you."

It was a shame that we couldn't join the raid... but we had to prioritize slaying the boss.

We couldn't leave him to roam the floor.

"Then we'll be taking our leave."

Before they left, I gave them a warning.

"But remember this."

"...?"

"If you fail, you'll be held responsible. You were the ones who refused my help."

His reply came after a moment of silence.

"...We understand."

He didn't sound sincere at all.

We left the thornreed field.

There was no point in staying and watching.

「The Chaos Lord, Riakis, draws upon the power of the source.」

If Riakis used his ultimate skill, it would only cause more trouble.

We couldn't risk being targeted as his 'prey'.

'And if they fail, they'll try to blame us.'

It was better to leave them to their own devices.

"Bjorn! Where are we going?"

"Somewhere with people."

"...Huh?"

"It seems like this raid will take a while."

Amelia seemed curious, so I explained.

"Hmm, why do you think so?"

"First, the timing."

"The timing?"

"Yes, if they were confident in their abilities, they would have summoned him earlier. They wouldn't wait until the 13th day."

They were slaying the Floor Lord without permission.

The best strategy was to summon him, slay him quickly, and then escape before the floor closed.

But they had waited until the last minute?

It meant they were expecting a long battle.

"...What's the second reason?"

"They had a Purification Torch on their belts."

"The Purification Torch... you mean the 8,000-series Numbered Item?"

It was No. 8645, to be precise.

If all the raid members had it, they could skip Riakis's 2nd phase.

"But why is that a problem?"

"Because it forces you to fight a long battle. It makes the raid easier overall, but it's not ideal if you want to slay him quickly."

I had talked too much, so I quickly added,

"...That's what I read in a book."

"Oooooh! Amazing!"

Thankfully, Ainar didn't notice anything strange. She was used to me spouting random knowledge.

"Ahem... Anyway! It seems like this might take a while, so we should focus on rescuing the other explorers. Is everyone okay with that?"

"Of course!"

"Yes, it's true that even if they manage to hold off Riakis, there will be casualties if this drags on. I agree."

"I don't mind, since we can't go up anyway... But why are you asking us? You've already made up your mind."

"It's procedure! Procedure!"

Okay, so everyone agreed.

"...I-I agree too!"

We started exploring the 3rd floor, and everywhere we went, explorers recognized me and asked for help.

Some of them were strong enough to handle the Chaos Spirits on their own...

But that didn't matter.

"Hey, it's Baron Yandel!"

"The Giant, Bjorn Yandel...!"

"We're saved!"

They were all terrified of Riakis.

"Th-Thank you for saving us...!"

"Ooooooh! It's the Chieftain!!! The Chieftain has come to save us!!"

We spent about eight hours rescuing explorers, and soon we had over a hundred people gathered in one place.

I decided to set up a base camp.

We couldn't keep moving with this many people.

"Warriors, can you help me out?"

"Of course!!! Just tell us what to do!!"

We didn't have enough people to guard the entire perimeter, so I asked the barbarian warriors to help with security.

And then something unexpected happened.

"Baron! We want to help too! Give us something to do!"

"Me too! I want to help!"

The other explorers volunteered to help, and soon we had a fully functional defense system.

'...This is making my job too easy.'

As long as Riakis didn't come this way, we could defend the base with the current manpower.

So I sent Erwen and Amelia on a mission.

"Erwen, Amelia. Can you do me a favor?"

"Yes! Just trust me!"

"...I always end up doing the dirty work when I'm with you."

Their mission was simple.

To search the area and bring back any stranded or isolated explorers.

"Bjorn, what about me? What can I do?!"

Ainar, unfortunately, couldn't navigate the labyrinth on her own, so I put her in charge of base security.

And...

「Character's Fame has increased by +1.」

「Character's Fame has increased by +1.」

「Character's Fame has increased by +1...」

「...」

「...」

The 13th day, the 14th day...

Time flew by.

The day of the labyrinth closure had arrived.

"Mister, I'm back!"

I woke up to Erwen's amber eyes.

"Good work. How many did you bring this time?"

"Thirty-one. We searched far and wide, but there's no one left in the area."

"I see. Get some rest."

"Okay."

"...You're going to rest?"

"Isn't being next to you considered resting?"

"...."

Amelia didn't respond.

I checked the time.

[04:01]

About 18 hours until the labyrinth closed.

The lower-level explorers, who had been terrified, were starting to relax.

They felt safe, knowing that there were over a thousand explorers gathered in one place.

But I was getting more and more anxious.

'They're still raiding...'

The Chaos Lord was still a problem.

I had expected a long battle, but I didn't think it would take this long.

'The Chaos Spirits disappeared a few hours ago, so they must have finished the 2nd phase...'

Then how was the raid going?

I hoped they were doing well, despite my dislike for them.

If they failed, and Riakis entered his 3rd phase, it would be a disaster.

"Phew..."

I couldn't help but worry.

I patrolled the shelter, checking for any problems.

There were none.

Well, the Chaos Spirits had stopped spawning, so it was to be expected.

"...."

After finishing my patrol, we had a team meal.

It was 6:30 AM.

Kaboom!

We were eating when the ground shook.

"Bjorn, what was that...?"

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

Damn it.

"I-It's the Floor Lord..."

"He's here..."

"I-I heard that hundreds of people died last time..."

Fear and chaos spread through the shelter.

I stood up, took a deep breath, and shouted,

"Everyone, be quiet!!"

"...."

It worked.

I activated [Gigantification] so they could see me from afar.

Hundreds of eyes were on me.

I quickly asked Erwen,

"Is he coming this way?"

"...No."

That was a relief.

"...."

I looked around and called out to the first mage I saw.

"Lewis Siyur!"

It was the mage we had met at the Orc Encampment.

"...Y-Yes? Why me...?"

"You're in charge now."

"...W-What?! Me?!"

He seemed like a competent mage, based on our brief conversation after the Orc Lord incident.

"Lead these people to safety. As far away as possible."

"B-But what about you, Baron?!"

It was an obvious question.

"I'm going to stop him."

"W-What?! B-But Tersia said he wasn't coming this way..."

"So you're just going to let him roam free?"

The lower-level explorers in the shelter weren't the only ones in danger.

There were many more scattered throughout the 3rd floor.

If Riakis entered his 3rd phase and started roaming the floor, it would be a disaster.

"Baron..."

"Speak."

"You're... you're just like the rumors..."

What?

"I'll take that as a compliment."

I grabbed my hammer and headed towards the direction Erwen had indicated. My teammates followed me.

Thud, thud.

We ran, our steps heavy with urgency.

The lower-level explorers parted like the Red Sea, making way for us.

And...

"May the blessings of Lafdonia be with you..."

...they bowed their heads in respect.

"Lewis! What are you doing?! Hurry up and lead them away!"

I shouted to the mage I had put in charge, but he didn't answer.

He just bowed his head.

"...."

I felt a strange sense of awkwardness and increased my pace.

Amelia chuckled.

"You're embarrassed, aren't you?"

I thought about it for a moment.

Why should I be embarrassed?

There aren't many high-level explorers who would go this far to protect lower-level explorers.

Especially not a noble.

'They should be grateful. It's not like I'm asking for anything in return.'

That thought made me feel better.

Thud, thud.

We left the shelter and started running.

I regretted my decision.

'I should have insisted on joining the raid.'

I could have held off Riakis in his 1st phase by myself.

Even if I was targeted as his 'prey', I could have outrun him until the floor closed.

But the 3rd phase was a different story.

'Well, at least it's not as bad as Ice Rock.'

There was a silver lining.

'Is he in his 3rd phase, or 4th phase?'

If it was his 3rd phase, we wouldn't be able to do anything but run.

'I hope it's his 4th phase.'

If they had failed the raid, and Riakis was in his final phase...

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

I saw Riakis in the distance and stopped running.

And I smiled.

'He looks skinny.'

This was doable.

"Get ready."

"...You're going to fight him?"

I couldn't just let him escape.

Chapter 460: Raid (5)

The Floor Lord.

A field boss that appears on each floor of the labyrinth when specific conditions are met.

It's usually considered a high-level raid, something you attempt only after you're strong enough to clear 7th-floor rifts.

At least, that's the case for the first five floors.

From the 6th floor onwards, the Floor Lord becomes a 'top-tier raid', practically end-game content.

'But this is our first real raid, and it's a Floor Lord...'

Considering that our previous highest-level raid was the 4th-floor 'Doppelganger Forest', this was an absurd situation.

And we only had four people who could fight.

We weren't even a full party, let alone a raid team.

But...

'We have several 3rd-grade essences, so we should be able to land the final blow.'

I made my decision as soon as I saw Riakis in the distance.

It would be foolish not to try.

And besides...

'I made a promise.'

It might sound childish, but I had made a promise to myself.

That if I ever saw him again...

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

...things would be different.

"Yandel, what are we going to do?"

"I'll tank the damage, you guys focus on attacking."

"That's... it?"

Amelia looked at me as if I was insane, but that was the only way to fight Riakis in his 4th phase.

There was no special strategy.

You just had to overpower him.

「The Chaos Lord, Riakis, senses the source of life.」

As soon as we entered his range, Riakis stopped running and turned to face us.

And...

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

He charged at us, roaring.

His footsteps were different from his 1st phase.

Thud, thud, thud!

They were still heavy, but much faster and lighter.

His speed was also incomparable.

His 4th phase was all about agility.

"Everyone, move back."

I sent my teammates to the back and activated [Transcendence] and [Gigantification].

「Size increases proportionally to Strength.」

If it had been his 1st phase, when he was bloated and obese, it wouldn't have been enough... but now that he was lean and muscular, our sizes were similar.

That was one of the advantages of [Gigantification].

「The Chaos Lord, Riakis, senses a great threat from the character.」

It significantly increased my 'Threat' stat, making it easier to hold aggro during raids.

He must have been surprised.

He had probably never encountered an opponent his size before.

"Behel—laaaaaaaaaa!"

I activated [Wild Release], further increasing my Threat stat, and charged at him.

The Giant Shield Charge.

Kwaaang!

The giant barbarian and the giant monster collided, creating a deafening roar.

Screech.

I was pushed back.

It wasn't surprising.

The Floor Lords were notorious for their ridiculous stats.

Thud!

I swung my hammer, activating [Swing]... but he didn't even flinch.

He should have been stunned, at least.

He immediately retaliated with a swipe of his claw.

[Kyiieeeeeeek!]

His attack speed was incredible in his 4th phase, and I couldn't dodge it.

Well, I didn't have to.

Thud!

I was tough too.

My hammer didn't faze him, and his claw didn't hurt me—

'...Why am I getting dizzy?'

I quickly reassessed the situation.

My Resistance stats were high enough to prevent my head from being ripped off or my body from being shredded...

But...

'...I can't take another hit to the head.'

The impact was making me dizzy.

It was worse than getting slapped by my gym teacher in middle school.

"Bjorn!!"

I shook off the nostalgic memory and focused on the present.

Whoosh!

He was already swinging his claw again.

Kwaaang!

This time, I blocked it with my shield.

And my teammates had finally caught up.

「Amelia Rainwales has cast [Self-Replication].」

Amelia charged at Riakis from both sides, accompanied by her clone.

Slash-

She activated [Abyssal Strength] without hesitation, focusing her attacks on his lower body.

And at the same time...

「Ainar Frenelin has cast [Reaping Slash].」

Ainar leaped into the air and brought her greatsword down on Riakis's shoulder.

But there was no explosion.

This wasn't a mob hunt.

「Ainar Frenelin has cast [Wild Control].」

[Wild Control].

The essence I had recommended to Ainar when she was deciding on her reward.

The effect of this skill was simple.

「Converts all conditional activation effects of the next attack to Cleaving Power.」

[Explosive Scars], [Mandel Oil], [Residual Heat].

The damage from all three of her conditional skills was converted to Cleaving Power.

In other words...

Slash!

...she could freely switch between raid mode and hunting mode.

Of course, it wasn't a critical hit.

She had only managed to cut through his thick hide, drawing a few drops of black blood.

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!]

Amelia's attacks were effective because she was an Aura user, but Ainar's damage was also surprisingly high.

Now all that was left was Erwen...

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia has cast [Focused Fire].」

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia has cast [Elemental Fusion].」

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia has cast [Rupture].」

And she didn't disappoint.

"Mister, dodge!"

I knew her well, so I immediately moved to the side, giving her a clear shot.

And...

Flash.

A beam of light shot through the air.

Zwoong!

It was followed by a compressed sound of air being torn apart.

Kwaaang!

[Grooooaaaaaaaaar!!!]

I smiled as I saw Riakis roaring in pain.

Erwen's attack wasn't a critical hit...

'But she managed to crack his forehead.'

Her damage was enough.

Therefore...

「The Chaos Lord, Riakis, has cast [Tainted Horn].」

The raid had officially begun.

The Chaos Lord, Riakis.

His 1st phase was simple.

He would inflict continuous damage with his passive aura, [Rain of Despair], and the Chaos Spirits would corrupt the terrain, causing additional damage. Corrupted tiles would spawn orbs that healed Riakis.

There was only one thing to be wary of.

[Crack of Evil].

His instant-kill skill, activated after he consumed a certain number of orbs.

The solution was simple.

You just had to escape the area of effect.

The problem was that the designated 'prey' would be immobilized if they left the area.

Riakis would then focus his attacks on the 'prey', and if the 'prey' was slain, his stats would increase, making the next phase more difficult.

'Well, at least his 1st phase is mostly about tanking.'

The real raid started in his 2nd phase.

Once his HP dropped below a certain threshold, Riakis would feel threatened and open the [Gate of Chaos], dragging everyone inside.

All tiles would be permanently corrupted.

Mental corruption would be severe.

And the players would have to track down Riakis in that hellish environment and fight him with his enhanced stats.

It was the most difficult phase of the raid.

But there was a way to make it easier.

The 'Purification Torch'.

You just had to activate it and wait.

After two days in game time, the [Gate of Chaos] would reopen, and you would return to the original field.

'It's easier to just get enough Purification Torches for everyone, even if it's a bit of a hassle.'

Of course, there was a drawback to this strategy.

If you skipped the 3rd phase, Riakis would recover all his HP and be even stronger.

Well, it was still more efficient to use the torches, so I had always used them in the past.

Anyway.

In his 3rd phase, the Chaos Spirits would stop spawning.

But [Rain of Despair] would be enhanced.

The rain would quickly corrupt the terrain, disabling all equipment.

Numbered Items would lose their effects until the labyrinth closed, and Adamantium armor would become as weak as ordinary steel.

'That's probably where the Sawtooth Clan started to fall apart.'

You couldn't use the Purification Torch to resist the mental corruption anymore.

And you had to fight a stronger Riakis in that state.

I guessed that the Sawtooth Clan had used a rotation strategy. They would have taken turns tanking the damage and slowly whittled down his HP.

'That's the only way he could have survived for six hours in his 3rd phase.'

But I wasn't interested in how they had failed the raid.

Something must have gone wrong.

That's how [Dungeon and Stone] worked.

One small mistake could lead to disastrous consequences.

'But they managed to reach his 4th phase, so they must have been prepared.'

It had worked out for us in the end.

The most difficult part of his 4th phase was that equipment bonuses were nullified, and we didn't have that penalty.

'I can't just let this opportunity pass.'

It was like they were handing us the victory on a silver platter.

Kwaaang!

After over 30 minutes of fighting, Erwen's arrow pierced Riakis's left eye.

「The Chaos Lord, Riakis, has cast [Tainted Left Eye].」

Okay, so both his eyes were gone now.

Whoosh!

Black smoke poured from his empty eye sockets.

And a chilling glow emanated from within.

[...]

He didn't even groan anymore, even when his flesh was pierced or his eyes were destroyed.

He was becoming less and less human.

'His claws regenerated... and he's already used seven Tainted Body Parts...'

His 4th phase was simple.

You just had to keep attacking him, and he would use his Tainted Body Parts to heal and become stronger.

And...

'Eleven down.'

One more to go.

He could use twelve of his 36 body parts.

"Everyone, move back!"

I sent my teammates to a safe distance, and Riakis unleashed his final attack.

「The Tainted Power is going berserk.」

His berserk pattern, a staple of boss monsters.

It was a race against time.

「Blessing of the Pilgrim.」

「The unknown power hidden within the chaos begins to purify the Tainted Power.」

It would take about five minutes for Riakis to cleanse his Tainted Body Parts and return to his normal state.

We had to corrupt one more body part before that—

"M-Mister!"

Erwen called out to me.

What was wrong? There shouldn't be any problems in the backline at this point...

"T-There are people coming this way! Dozens of them! And they're fast!"

"What?"

Dozens of people? Who?

Were the barbarians from the shelter worried about me and coming to help?

That was my first thought.

But...

"...I think it's the Sawtooth Clan!"

The world wasn't that kind.

"What?! But I thought they failed the raid!"

Ainar exclaimed, her voice filled with disbelief.

I was also confused.

'What's going on?'

Dozens of people?

If they had that many, why was Riakis roaming the floor alone? Had they actually succeeded in the raid?

I couldn't understand.

But one thing was certain.

'They're coming this way...'

It wasn't good news.

'They're here just as we're about to land the final blow...'

They wouldn't let us take the kill.

'...But that doesn't change anything.'

「Purification rate: 1%.」

About 10 minutes until Riakis fully recovered.

Let's just shorten that time.

Like a true barbarian.

"Behel—laaaaaaaaaa!"

"M-Mister?"

"What are you waiting for?! Kill him!!"

We'll worry about the aftermath later.

Chapter 461: Proof (1)

The berserk pattern lasted for about 10 minutes.

After that, Riakis would instantly recover, nullifying all our efforts.

But 10 minutes was a reasonable amount of time.

At least, in the world of [Dungeon and Stone], where unreasonable situations were the norm.

「Purification rate: 3%.」

We just had to land one more hit within 10 minutes.

Chapter 462: Proof (2)

Chapter 463: Proof (3)

[Chaos]

Doubles the amount of negative stats possessed, and increases all stats proportionally to the increased negative stats.

That's all it said in the game.

It was up to the players to figure out the formula.

"Negative stats... you mean debuffs?"

Ah, I had to explain that first.

"That's part of it, but not all of it."

Each stat in [Dungeon and Stone] had a coefficient, and that coefficient determined how much it would increase your Physical, Mental, or Ability stats.

For example, Dexterity had a coefficient of 0.5, so if your Dexterity increased by +1, your Ability stat would increase by 0.5.

'Magic Resistance, a high-value stat, has a coefficient of 3.'

Anyway, that wasn't important right now.

Most stats had a coefficient of 0 or higher, but some had negative coefficients.

I called them 'negative stats'.

"For example, you have Impulsiveness and Distrust."

These stats would decrease your overall stats if they had a positive value.

And if a stat with a positive coefficient had a negative value, it would be considered a 'debuff', like the -30 Control from [Rupture].

"Ah..."

Erwen seemed to understand the basic concept, so I started calculating.

What was Erwen's current negative stat value?

First, -80 Physical Resistance from the 'Evan' essence.

Then, -40 Soul Regeneration from the 'Lithcannon' essence I had recommended as a reward.

-30 Control from [Rupture].

'+30 Impulsiveness and +30 Distrust from 'Kalban'.

'Greed from the 'Metcrow' essence has a coefficient of 0, so we don't need to include that. And I removed the +40 Deficiency before entering the labyrinth...'

I multiplied each stat by its coefficient, added them together, and then multiplied the sum by the Chaos Essence's coefficient of 0.2.

'53.8.'

That was the total stat increase Erwen had gained from the Chaos Essence.

Well, not all of her stats.

Stats like Luck and Bone Density, which she didn't possess, wouldn't increase.

'...She's already getting a lot out of it.'

I was impressed.

I had recommended the 'Lithcannon' essence, but the rest were all essences she had chosen herself.

'It's a bit risky, considering how much her Mental stats are being reduced...'

But she had grown mentally stronger.

She was much calmer now, even though the essence's effects were stronger than before.

'I should replace it with a different essence soon.'

Mental stats had low coefficients, so they weren't ideal for maximizing the Chaos Essence's potential. Control was decent, but 'Distrust' only had a coefficient of 0.6.

It was more efficient to reduce high-value stats with high coefficients. If you could reduce Magic Resistance by 50, you could increase all your other stats by 30.

'...Well, technically it's a 100 reduction, considering the doubled negative stat effect.'

Anyway, that was why the Chaos Essence was so popular among damage dealers.

Tank essences rarely had negative stats that reduced damage and increased defense.

"I see... You said it was better to specialize, right...?"

Hmm, did I say that?

I couldn't remember, but it was time to move on.

"Show me your abilities."

Even though we weren't in the usual inn room, we still had to test her skills.

The Chaos Essence came with two skills.

One passive and one active.

That was all.

It seemed lacking compared to the Guardian essences, which could give you up to five active skills...

But sometimes, one powerful skill was better than a hundred mediocre ones.

(P) Chaos Circuit — All resources possessed by the character are combined into one. When all resources are depleted, the character can use the power of Chaos.

The passive skill was unique.

It combined all your resources, such as mana, natural energy, Soul Power, and divine power, into one gauge.

That alone made it incredibly valuable.

Natural energy, which was required for spirit magic, was incredibly difficult to increase.

'That's probably why Erwen absorbed the Ertes essence. She could use its passive to convert Soul Power into natural energy.'

I made a mental note to remove that essence when we got back to the city. [Elemental Fusion] wasn't a bad skill, but it wasn't worth keeping in the long run.

"So... about my abilities..."

"[Focused Fire]. Use that. We need to deplete your resources anyway. And summon your spirit too."

"...Wouldn't it be faster to summon Dicloe? He'll drain my energy much faster."

Oh, she was right.

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia has summoned [Dicloe, the Dark Spirit King].」

A black mass emerged from a spatial rift, taking the form of a humanoid figure.

Dicloe, at Erwen's command, fired a barrage of dark orbs at the opposite wall.

Kaboom! Kaboom! Kaboom!

The barrier protecting the cabin didn't even flinch.

Erwen's resources were depleted after about 30 seconds.

It used to be 5 seconds when she used it at full power.

The [Chaos Circuit] activated.

The Infinite Power Mode.

The longer she charged it, the more MP it consumed...

Whoosh!

It was more efficient to use the 60-second cooldown and fire another arrow.

1 second, 2 seconds, 3 seconds, 4 seconds...

One, two, three, four...

By the time the vote was over, we had barely reached a majority.

"...S-Seventeen votes in favor. The proposal to officially support Baron Yandel and Clan Anabada has been approved."

Even the Rabbit Baroness, a Melbeth veteran, was surprised.

But my excitement faded as the discussion continued.

"We agree to support you, but we won't use Melbeth's funds."

They all agreed to support me, but they placed restrictions on the funding.

And in the end, I was left with a meager three options.

1. Take out a loan from Melbeth (with interest and a limit).

2. Use one of Melbeth's buildings as our clan house (at a discounted price).

3. Accept Melbeth's exclusive quests.

The most important one was the third option.

It meant that Melbeth would officially request our help if they needed us, and we would be compensated accordingly.

'...Well, it's a start.'

I decided to be satisfied with this for now.

After the meeting, I returned home in the carriage.

I needed to get some sleep.

"Ah, Bjorn... You're here..."

"Good morning, Shabin Emure."

"...Is it morning already?"

"...."

I went straight to the sanctuary and started working.

I checked on the progress of the real estate project, approved requests from the administrative team, and...

"Oh! Bjorn! You're here!"

"It's Chieftain."

"...Right, Chieftain!"

I met with Ainar and discussed the recent events in the tribe.

How many of the new warriors had survived their first expedition.

What was the general mood.

And...

"M-Me? The 2nd Elder...?"

"Do you have a problem with that?"

"No way! I'll do it! I'll do whatever you ask!!"

I appointed Charon, the warrior who had been betrayed by his teammate and then started the Backpack Revolution, as the 2nd Elder.

Ainar needed help.

We had to prepare for next month's coming-of-age ceremony, and I couldn't handle everything myself.

And it would be too much to ask the administrative team to do it.

One day, two days...

Time flew by.

And then a new day arrived.

July 5th, 157th year of the New Era.

The day we started selling land.

Chapter 466: Land Owner (1)

The first real estate sale in barbarian history was about to begin. Ainar brought me good news, and a smile spread across my face.

"130 gathered?"

"Yes! They're all clamoring, asking when the sale starts!"

The number of prospective buyers was much higher than I'd anticipated. I quickly turned to the Minister of Urban Development for the Barbarian Tribe.

"Rick, how many plots can we sell today?"

"73."

One plot of land was 400 square meters, or roughly 1,300 square feet.

'So we have about 30,000 square feet available for sale today.'

Rick had surveyed the land by himself, and I could only imagine how much work he'd put in.

"But... are you sure this unit of measurement is okay? One plot...?"

"Don't worry. This will be much easier for them to understand."

If we used larger units, it would only confuse the barbarians.

It was much simpler to define one plot as a 20-meter by 20-meter square.

Of course, I planned to offer smaller and larger plots later, to give buyers more options.

200 square meters would be half a plot.

600 square meters would be one and a half plots.

Much simpler, right?

"Anyway, we'll only sell 40 plots today."

"...What? We're not selling them all?"

"Not yet."

I had prepared as much land as possible, just in case, but selling it all at once would disrupt my future plans.

"...Understood."

Rick, who had worked tirelessly for this day, looked a little disappointed, but it couldn't be helped. The key to real estate was future value.

"Alright, let's go."

After adjusting the number of lots in the lottery box, we headed to the sales location. It was a forest near the outer gate, convenient for travel to and from the city.

"Oh, it's the Chieftain!"

"Start selling! I've brought enough money!"

The warriors who spotted me started calling out.

I greeted them briefly, scanning their equipment as I did.

'Their average level is around 5th floor.'

They were among the higher-ranking warriors in the tribe.

They needed to be at least this strong to afford the 2 million stone price tag I'd set for each plot. Barbarians weren't known for their saving habits.

Ainar had told me that some warriors had even sold their prized equipment to buy land.

"Greetings, warriors who seized the opportunity!"

I climbed onto the platform Ainar had prepared and began my sales pitch.

"Do you see those four yellow flags over there?"

"Yes!"

"The land within those flags is what we're selling today."

I needed to explain the concept first. Some of the warriors who had come might just be curious onlookers.

I needed to stimulate their desire to buy.

"It's quite spacious, isn't it? There's still room even with over a hundred of us here!"

"Uh... is it?"

"Think about the inn rooms you stay in! They're barely big enough for a single bed! This plot of land can easily fit a hundred beds!"

Of course, inn rooms had walls and a roof, unlike this open space... but that wasn't important right now.

"Hmm... Now that you mention it, it does seem spacious!"

Good.

"Yes, it's a vast land! You can build a house here, and turn the rest into a training ground. And since it's private property, you can even use the abilities you can't use in the city!"

"...Private property?"

"It means it's completely yours!"

"...Oooooh!"

The warriors seemed impressed by the fancy term.

"Ah, and by the way, land this size would cost hundreds of millions of stones in the city. And commoners can't even buy it!"

I casually omitted the fact that such prices only applied to Karnon, the Imperial City, and continued to stimulate their desire to buy.

And when the time was right...

"Ahem!"

I cleared my throat and got to the main point.

"Anyway, we're selling 40 plots today, all the same size as this one!"

"...Forty plots? But there are over a hundred of us here?"

The barbarians, summoned early in the morning and further motivated by my speech, complained about the limited supply.

But I just doubled down.

"Stop complaining! Land is a precious resource! We barely managed to secure 40 plots! If you don't want it, don't buy it!"

Limited supply meant the seller had the advantage.

The warriors immediately shut their mouths, and I continued as planned.

"Now, each of you will come forward and draw a lot! Those who draw a black lot will have the opportunity to buy land!"

We divided the 130 warriors into six groups, and they took turns drawing lots. Soon, cheers and groans filled the air.

"I got a black lot! A black lot!!"

"A white one?! This is unfair! I drew last! Let me draw again!!"

But complaining wouldn't change the outcome.

After the lottery, the winners were led to Rick Anderson, who handled the sales and collected the payments.

"Hey, human... I can't read!"

"Don't worry. It's the Chieftain's land. It's all legitimate."

"...Is it?"

The deed stated that the land could only be inherited by other barbarians, and if there were no heirs, the ownership would revert to the tribe...

But that was a minor detail.

"Now, just press your thumbprint here."

The barbarians happily pressed their inked thumbprints onto the documents.

And...

'80 million stones from selling undeveloped land... not bad.'

The average income from the coming-of-age ceremony was about 10 million stones, so this was enough to cover eight ceremonies.

"Hey! What are you doing?! This is my land! Get out!"

"S-Sorry! I was just looking around..."

"Just for a moment. And don't even breathe while you're here."

"Understood!"

The warriors who had bought land started setting up their tents, while those who hadn't looked on enviously.

"Shrive's son, Byukan! I'll give you 100,000 stones more. Sell me your land."

"Hmm, 100,000 stones...?"

The situation I had anticipated was already unfolding.

Well, the deal didn't go through in the end.

"Sorry, but that's not enough."

"I see... Let me know if you change your mind."

The warrior, reluctant to pay such a high premium, backed down.

But that didn't matter.

"Ainar."

I called her name, and she nodded resolutely, disappearing into the crowd.

I watched her leave and sighed.

"Phew..."

There was no turning back now.

The day after the forty plots were sold out,

A rumor swept through the sanctuary.

"Did you hear? Shrive's son, Byukan, sold his land for 3 million stones!"

"He earned a million stones in a single day!"

"What?! A million stones?! That's enough to buy a Laithium breastplate! The one the Chieftain wears!"

The land that had been sold for 2 million stones had doubled in value in a single day.

Of course, it wasn't a normal transaction.

"Is that guy insane? I mean, the Chieftain's words made sense, but... 3 million stones for a plot of land?!"

"Who bought it?"

The buyer was Bytol's second son, Seirik.

A member of the 'Comrade Association', a secret organization within the tribe created by Charon, the 2nd Elder who had started the Backpack Revolution.

"...I heard he made a fortune from selling backpacks... but I didn't know he had that much money!"

I had secretly funded Charon, instructing him to choose a trustworthy warrior with a tight lip.

The reason was simple.

"But... maybe the Chieftain was right..."

No matter how much I talked about the value of land, not all barbarians would believe me.

"You mean when he said the land would become too expensive to buy later?"

"Yes! I heard he's selling more land this month! I'm thinking about buying some too."

"Hmm, I thought you didn't need land. What changed your mind?"

"I still don't need land! But think about it. We can buy it now and sell it later for a profit! It's like getting a free weapon! You should buy some too!"

"Hmm... I'd rather save the money and buy a weapon! At least weapons are useful in battle. Land is just..."

There were still many warriors who were skeptical, but that was just a matter of time.

The land that had been sold for 2 million stones was resold for 3.5 million.

And then 3.7 million.

And then 4 million.

All thanks to our 'comrade'.

"...W-What?! 4 million stones for a plot of land?! Are they all insane?!"

In just four days, the sanctuary was in an uproar.

"Why aren't they selling?! I'll pay 4 million stones!"

Some barbarians were eager to buy, but no one was selling.

They weren't fools.

"It'll be worth even more tomorrow! Why would I sell it now?!"

"B-But...!"

The next day, the land was sold for 4 million stones, and the warriors were hooked.

It wasn't just the barbarians who were caught up in the frenzy.

Even the modern clerks, trained in economics, were starting to lose their minds.

"4.3 million! I'll buy it for 4.3 million!!"

In just a week, the market had become self-sustaining.

I no longer needed to rely on 'comrades'.

I instructed Charon to continue buying land until the 50 million stones I had given him were all spent. It was two-thirds of the profit, but I didn't mind.

'The next batch of land will sell for 6 million stones each.'

Why the price increase?

It was simple.

The new plots were closer to the city center, so of course they were more expensive.

And the barbarians would buy them anyway.

After all, they believed that buying land was a guaranteed profit.

'Okay, then I don't have to worry about this anymore...'

I was about to leave the chieftain's tent when Rick Anderson, who was in charge of the real estate project, approached me.

"This is strange, isn't it?"

"What is?"

"The land prices. I don't see any reason why they should be increasing. The sanctuary isn't that small, you know. Mathematically speaking, even if every warrior owned a plot of land, there would still be land left over."

"Hmm, is that so?"

"Yes. The warriors who bought land will regret it later."

"Why should I stop them? They're the ones who want to buy it."

"That's... true."

"Anyway, I'm leaving!"

Finally, I could go home.

I rushed back to the city, expecting to be scolded by Amelia and Erwen... but there were no complaints.

"Amelia, Erwen! I'm—"

"Read this."

Amelia tossed me an open envelope.

"It's from Mozelan."

"...?"

I felt a sense of unease as I read the letter.

It was written in formal language, befitting a letter from Mozelan, the noble's police force. It was unnecessarily long, but the first three lines were enough.

[...This is a formal request from Count Alminus, acting as a representative of the Sawtooth Clan, to the Mozelan Dispute Resolution Committee...]

Who had filed the complaint.

[...We demand compensation of 2.135 billion stones, half the appraised value of the Chaos Lord Riakis essence...]

What they wanted.

And...

[...Baron Bjorn Yandel is requested to appear at Mozelan Headquarters within 15 days of receiving this letter.]

When I had to go.

I tore the letter to shreds and swallowed it.

'I've never received a lawsuit in my life, and now I'm getting one here?'

It was ironic, but I wasn't scared.

I had been in this world for four years now.

'Count Alminus...'

I had never lost a fight.

At least not to someone who had more to lose than me.

Chapter 467: Land Owner (2)

House Alminus. It wasn't one of the great houses that had existed since the founding of the Lafdonia Kingdom. However...

[Alminus Central Exchange]

[Alminus Bank]

...the Alminus Trading Company, which owned these institutions, wielded influence comparable to the oldest noble families. They were also patrons of the Sawtooth Clan, one of the four largest explorer clans, extending their reach into the labyrinth.

"I knew they wouldn't stay quiet, but I didn't expect them to make it a public issue with Mozelan."

Amelia chuckled, seemingly agreeing with my muttered comment.

"The Count is bolder than I thought. I didn't expect him to resort to this, especially considering the circumstances surrounding the unauthorized raid."

"Emily, it's not your fault, so don't feel responsible."

"...Responsible? Why should I feel responsible?"

"Uh..."

I just thought it was the right thing to say...

Well, whatever.

I quickly changed the subject, feeling a little awkward.

"Anyway, the letter was sent two days ago. Why didn't you tell me sooner?"

"I knew you were busy, and I had a few things to check first. If you hadn't come home today, I was planning to go find you."

"Things to check?"

Amelia then told me what she had been doing for the past two days. It wasn't good news.

"First, it seems like they've already started spreading rumors in the taverns."

House Alminus had begun their smear campaign.

"I can guess what they're saying, but... what exactly are the rumors?"

"They're all slightly different, but the gist of it is that we somehow managed to steal the Floor Lord they were raiding. Some even speculate that we were the ones who summoned him in the first place."

"As expected..."

"But there are also those who dismiss the rumors. It seems like your reputation isn't completely worthless."

Right, it was thanks to the Fame I had accumulated.

I felt a little proud, but...

"We still need to hurry."

"Huh?"

"I saw them approaching the explorers you helped that day. They're trying to bribe them into giving favorable testimonies."

"...Bribe them? But they can't lie because of the Verification spell."

"Are you unaware? You can plead the Fifth in Mozelan trials. They'll probably instruct them to avoid answering any unfavorable questions and only reveal the parts that benefit them."

"...Hmm, but wouldn't our testimony be more credible?"

"Not necessarily. In cases where testimonies contradict each other, the court often gives less weight to the testimonies themselves. It's a common strategy."

Oh... I see...

"But... how do you know all this?"

"I studied the relevant documents for the past two days."

"..."

Maybe Amelia's sister wasn't the genius of the family, after all.

Impressed by her thoroughness, I asked for her advice.

"So what do you think I should do?"

"The trial is inevitable, so we need to find witnesses who will testify on our behalf. Among the explorers you helped that day."

"And?"

"Ask Melbeth for help. They've been around for a long time, they must have experienced similar disputes. They should have a competent lawyer."

"Okay, and then?"

I looked at her expectantly, and Amelia averted her gaze, seemingly burdened by my question.

"...I'm good at investigating, not finding solutions."

Right, she didn't have any other ideas.

"Is there anything I can do to help?"

Erwen, who had been quietly listening, finally spoke up.

I appreciated her offer, but...

"I'll let you know if I need your help. Don't worry about it for now."

I had enough to do as Chieftain. It was time to focus on my role as Clan Master.

After four days of nonstop work, I finally had some time off.

I wanted to stay home and relax, but I immediately left for Mozelan Headquarters.

Strike while the iron is hot.

I needed to gather information before formulating a strategy.

I wasn't particularly worried about the trial itself.

The only thing that bothered me was...

'...Why did they have to summon me to Headquarters?'

The Mozelan branch where Dwarkey had been detained was located in the 7th District, but Headquarters was in Karnon, the Imperial City.

'...Should I just use the military transfer station?'

I was tempted, but I ultimately decided against it. As Clan Master of Anabada, I couldn't be so wasteful.

It was expensive.

And I could sleep on the way there.

"Welcome to Mozelan Headquarters, Baron Yandel. This way, please. We'll escort you."

As soon as I arrived, the knights greeted me politely.

It was a strange feeling.

The last time I had visited Mozelan, they wouldn't even let me inside. And that wasn't even Headquarters, but a branch office.

Anyway, that wasn't important right now.

"I'm Christina Bealon, the Chair of Mozelan."

The head of Mozelan was a middle-aged woman with a refined appearance. She was dressed in casual clothes, but she was clearly a knight.

"Bealon...? Are you from House Bealon?"

"Yes. Count Bealon is my older brother."

"I see... But isn't it unusual for the Chair to handle this kind of matter personally?"

"Normally, it wouldn't be. However, disputes between titled nobles are a sensitive issue. I decided to handle this personally to avoid any mistakes."

"I see. So... what did you want to talk about? The letter said you would explain everything in person."

"Yes, some things are difficult to discuss in writing. Hmm, where should I begin..."

She looked at me cautiously and then started explaining the situation. As expected of a noble, her introduction was lengthy. She basically just reiterated the contents of the letter in more detail.

"...In cases like this, we usually recommend a settlement. Count Alminus has also expressed his willingness to lower the requested amount if you agree."

She paused and looked at me.

"But... I assume that's not what you want?"

Obviously.

"Of course not."

"Then we have no choice but to transfer the case to the Royal Court."

She then explained the procedures involved in a Royal Court trial.

I would have to write a statement...

"We can summon a scribe if you wish."

...but I decided to postpone it.

I needed to consult with a lawyer and formulate a strategy before writing anything.

So I left Mozelan Headquarters and went straight to Baroness Lirivia's mansion.

"Baron Yandel...? I wasn't informed of your visit..."

The Baroness's daughter greeted me.

Unfortunately, the Baroness was currently at the royal palace for official business. She would be back later today, so I decided to wait.

And after a few hours...

"Baron Yandel, what brings you here? Minia tells me you've been waiting for half a day..."

"Baroness Lirivia, I need your help."

"Help...? Please explain."

The Baroness, who had returned as the sun began to set, listened to my story and sighed.

"I don't mean to offend you, but... you really are a storm in human form."

"..."

"I know a competent lawyer, so don't worry too much. It's too late today, but you should be able to meet him tomorrow."

"Thank you. Do you have a spare room, by any chance?"

"A spare room...?"

"It's a bit of a hassle to go back to Ravigion and come back tomorrow."

"...You're staying in Ravigion? I understand. I'll have someone prepare a room for you."

"Thank you."

Okay, so lodging was taken care of.

I had dinner with the Baroness's family, and then I borrowed a recording crystal to inform Erwen and Amelia that I wouldn't be coming home tonight.

And the next morning...

"It's a pleasure to meet you, Baron Yandel. I'm James Elk."

I met with the lawyer the Baroness had recommended.

He listened to my story and frowned.

"...This is a complicated situation. Let me summarize. The formal charge filed by House Alminus is 'Request for loot distribution based on contribution', correct?"

"That's right."

They were spreading rumors about me behind my back, but the official charge was different.

"They're claiming that the Sawtooth Clan contributed to the raid, and therefore they're entitled to a share of the loot."

The basis for their claim was the Labyrinth Disaster Law.

During a disaster, all explorers were obligated to cooperate and resolve the situation. And disputes over loot distribution were a common occurrence.

Therefore, laws had been established to handle such situations.

"...This will be difficult..."

"...Is it that bad?"

"Yes. A request for loot distribution based on contribution is a legitimate claim under labyrinth law. And the fact that they filed a formal complaint means they have evidence to support their claim."

The lawyer looked at me and said,

"I recommend accepting Count Alminus's offer. Even if the case goes to trial, the court will likely rule in their favor, and you might end up paying even more."

"...Even though there's evidence suggesting that they summoned the Floor Lord without permission?"

My voice was sharper than I intended.

But the lawyer remained calm and professional.

"That's irrelevant to this case. It's just circumstantial evidence. It would be difficult to prove, and even if you could, it would be a separate case."

In other words, even if House Alminus was fined or imprisoned for the unauthorized summoning, they would still be entitled to a share of the loot.

"I'm just stating the facts, so please don't misunderstand."

Right, it wasn't his fault.

"James, is there really no other way?"

"As I said, I recommend a settlement. You can use the 'circumstantial evidence' you mentioned to negotiate a lower amount. It would minimize the risk."

"What if I don't care about the risk? Is there really no other way?"

"Yes. This is the best option. You can't avoid paying them. Unless Count Alminus withdraws his complaint."

I see...

That was it.

I ended the consultation.

I hadn't gained much from it, but it had given me a direction.

'I need to make him withdraw his complaint.'

In other words, I needed leverage.

After the consultation,

I went to Mozelan Headquarters and wrote my statement with the lawyer's help.

It was written in a way that would be most beneficial to me in court.

Avoiding a trial was the best option, but I had to be prepared for every possibility.

'The trial won't start for a few months...'

I still had plenty of time.

So I postponed my meeting with Count Alminus.

There was no point in meeting him now.

Therefore...

I went somewhere else.

"Welcome to the Explorer's Guild, Baron Yandel!"

I visited the Explorer's Guild and filed a raid request. The branch manager personally handled my request.

[Dreadfear, the Lord of Terror.]

The target was the 1st-floor Floor Lord.

"...It's been a while since we've had a Floor Lord raid request."

It wasn't surprising.

There were other ways for a 5th-grade clan to be promoted to 4th-grade.

Usually, only newly formed clans that were confident in their abilities would attempt a Floor Lord raid.

"The raid will take place during the next labyrinth opening... We'll send out a notice to all branches immediately."

The request was approved without any problems.

"You know, this will cause quite a stir."

Of course, that was my intention.

"A five-man raid team...? If they succeed, it will be the tenth such feat in history, after the Last Sage!"

This was my first bullet.

Aimed at Count Alminus.

Chapter 468: Land Owner (3)

House Alminus's first move wasn't the lawsuit, but a smear campaign.

Why?

They could have just filed the lawsuit and waited for the court to rule in their favor.

My conclusion was this:

'Count Alminus doesn't want this to go to trial.'

If that was true, the lawsuit was just a gesture.

Or rather, a threat.

It was a message: You and I are in different weight classes, so if you don't want to lose even more, settle this quietly and pay up.

'Count Alminus isn't short on money...'

So it must be about his reputation.

That's how the noble society worked.

It was a world divided into winners and losers.

And backing down in this situation would be a clear sign of defeat.

Therefore, I decided to turn public opinion in my favor.

That's what they were focusing on, after all.

'The original Baron Yandel wasn't strong enough to slay a Floor Lord.'

'If it weren't for the Sawtooth Clan, his clan wouldn't have been able to kill the Chaos Lord.'

Technically, both statements were true... but Count Alminus was using them to manipulate public opinion.

It was a setup.

He would start with questioning my abilities and then gradually escalate the accusations.

'Bjorn Yandel stole the credit from the Sawtooth Clan, who risked their lives to slay the Floor Lord.'

'Is that how a hero should act?'

Count Alminus had correctly identified the source of my power.

My reputation.

"Hmm, so you filed the raid request without telling us?"

"...Yes!"

I returned home and told Amelia about the raid plan. Her gaze was cold, but...

"Ha..."

...it didn't last long. She sighed, like a parent looking at their troublesome child, and asked,

"Do you even know how many clans have disappeared after attempting a five-man Floor Lord raid?"

"I heard about it. They said it's especially dangerous with only five people. That's why most raid teams have twenty to thirty members..."

That number was based on the average level of 6th and 7th-floor explorers.

But...

"Don't worry. I have a plan."

Five-man clears were the standard for the Lord of Terror.

That's how you got the best rewards.

And the rewards were well-known, so there were always a few clans who attempted it.

Not many, maybe two or three every hundred years.

'If you know the strategy, a five-man clear is actually much easier than a thirty-man raid.'

That's why I had chosen the Lord of Terror as our next raid target.

People considered a five-man Dreadfear clear more difficult than a Riakis clear. If we succeeded, no one would question my abilities.

"..."

Auyen, who had been quietly listening, started fidgeting.

I suddenly wondered why he was even here... but I didn't say anything.

He had been through a lot with us in Ice Rock.

"Lokrok."

"Yes, Baron. What can I do for you?"

I told him what he wanted to hear.

"You don't have to join us for this expedition. Don't worry about it."

"Ah... I see..."

He looked relieved, letting out a sigh.

"Then who will fill the last spot?"

After sending Auyen to his room, we discussed the raid plan and the remaining member.

And after a while...

"Yandel."

As I was about to leave the room, Amelia called out to me.

"You've been making a lot of enemies lately... Are you sure you can handle this?"

In other words, wasn't I being too reckless?

"The Count, for example. It would have been better to just pay him off than to make an enemy out of him."

I understood her concern.

They were the established powers of this world.

She was probably worried about the potential consequences of my actions.

Becoming Chieftain as a noble, challenging Count Alminus...

But...

"Amelia."

I didn't back down.

"If you're scared of being attacked, you'll never be able to expand your territory."

It was my long-held belief.

"...?"

Amelia looked at me as if I was speaking a different language.

The next day, I went out with Erwen.

It was just the two of us.

Our destination was Raven's lab in the Mage Tower.

"Oh, you're here?"

"It's been a while, Raven."

"I heard about your promotion. Congratulations. I tried to visit you a few times, but you were never home."

"I've been busy lately."

The lab was covered in dust, just like I remembered.

"...Sorry about the mess. I've been spending most of my time at the military base in the Imperial City. It's been a while since I've been here."

"Thank you. I didn't expect you to take time off for this."

"Of course I would. It's a chance to see a Floor Lord essence. Any mage would jump at the opportunity."

Well, she wasn't wrong.

But there was no one I trusted more than Raven when it came to magic.

Bersil specialized in artifacts, not essences.

"I did some research on the Riakis essence after you contacted me. It's quite a unique essence."

"It is."

"Shall we begin...?"

Raven approached Erwen with a grin, and Erwen took a step back, looking at me.

"...Can't we just do this with the two of us? Like before?"

Erwen didn't like being treated like a lab rat, but it couldn't be helped.

"As I said, we need a mage to properly analyze your new abilities."

"..."

"Can you do this?"

"Yes..."

Erwen reluctantly agreed, and Raven chuckled.

"...Erwen, you haven't changed at all."

"It's none of your business."

"Well, that's true. Come here."

The atmosphere was stifling.

We used to be so close...

But things had changed.

'She did try to separate us because she thought I was using Erwen...'

Maybe Raven still cared about me.

'Whatever, it's their problem.'

I didn't care about their relationship and focused on the experiment.

"Here, put this on."

Raven handed Erwen a familiar bracelet. It was the same one she had used when we tested the Corpse Golem essence.

Well, could you even call it a bracelet?

"...Ugh."

As soon as Erwen sat down on the ominous chair, the 'bracelet' snapped shut, locking her in place.

"Don't worry. We're just going to insert a mana conductor into your vein and monitor your vital signs."

"..."

"Shall we begin?"

Raven made the necessary preparations, and then she instructed Erwen to use her new ability.

"It might be difficult since it's your first time, but just imagine it. Try to remember the name the Sage gave it..."

"I can do it myself, so be quiet. I need to concentrate."

"Alright. You're the expert when it comes to abilities, after all."

Erwen closed her eyes, and a few seconds later...

Beep, beep, beep, beep...

The graph displaying Erwen's vital signs started fluctuating wildly.

The essence's skill had activated.

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia has cast [Fusion].」

[Fusion] was a Transformation ability, similar to my [Transcendence], but also different.

[Transcendence] affected active skills, while...

[Fusion] only affected passive skills.

I tried to remember Erwen's passive skills.

The 5th-grade Evan essence.

It increased all four elemental damage types: fire, water, wind, and earth.

The 4th-grade Lithcannon essence I had recommended as a reward.

It doubled the damage of casting skills.

「Chaos energy consumes [Harmony].」

The 5th-grade Patrol Ertes essence.

It converted Soul Power into natural energy.

「Chaos energy consumes [Half-Blood's Blessing].」

The 5th-grade Kalban essence.

It gave her a random buff every few minutes.

「Chaos energy consumes [Tracker's Eye].」

The 4th-grade Metcrow essence.

It tripled all vision-related stats and gave a bonus to ranged attacks.

And lastly...

「Chaos energy consumes [Food Chain].」

The 3rd-grade Heavenly Judge essence she had received from the fairy tribe after our team disbanded.

It increased damage against monsters proportional to their level.

Even though it wasn't as powerful as [Rupture], the essence's active skill, the passive was still quite valuable.

'The Floor Lord essence isn't included in the fusion, so that's all of them.'

I didn't know what the combined skill would be.

There were countless essences in [Dungeon and Stone].

Even I, a seasoned player, couldn't possibly test every single combination.

I hadn't played the cheat version.

'...Well, I've tested most of the higher-grade combinations.'

I had found quite a few useful combinations.

But recreating those combinations was a long-term goal.

So what about now?

What kind of skill had Erwen obtained?

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia's body is imbued with Chaos energy.」

We needed a mage to analyze the skill's effects.

I couldn't see the system logs.

"So... how does it feel? Anything different?"

"I don't know..."

"Alright, then we'll test it out one by one."

Raven began the experiment, just like she had done with me in the past.

It wasn't something we could finish in a single day.

"I've taken the rest of the month off, so come here every day."

Erwen's schedule for the month was set.

「Erwen Fornachi di Tersia's body is imbued with Chaos energy.」

In idle games, you have to click on the progress bars to speed things up.

Until they made a mistake and revealed their true intentions.

If the speaker believed their information was true, the jewel would glow green.

Chapter 474: Of Men and Women (1)
